‘Godzilla Minus Zero’ Chomping $6M In Previews, Higher Than ‘Predator: Badlands’; ‘Cat In The Hat’ $1M – Box Office Update

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Takashi Yamazaki’s R-rated Godzilla Minus Zero is roaring in the November box office to a great start with what we’re hearing is around $6 million in previews. That includes fan shows from Godzilla Day Tuesday, last night and tonight, the latter being $4M+. Today’s showtimes began at 10AM.

It’s very interesting that Godzilla Minus Zero gets an R: There aren’t any lizards who get naked in the movie, nor does any person or monster have a ‘F’ bomb tirade despite massive destruction in Japan as Godzilla and Ghidorah.

This is easily a preview record for Godzilla Minus Zero U.S. distributor GKIDs beating their previous high, 2023’s The Boy and the Heron which posted a Thursday and early access previews of $2.39M, and a 3-day of $13M. Early monies here on Godzilla Minus Zero are way above the $2.1M made by Yamazaki’s Godzilla Minus One from its Wednesday and Thursday showtimes which went on to an $11.4M opening. Watch on Deadline Related Stories News 'Godzilla Minus Zero' Stomping To $60M+ WW Opening, Posts Massive First Day In Japan; Will 'Ramayana' Upset The Lizard? - Box Office Preview News 'Godzilla Minus Zero' Review: Oscar Winner Takashi Yamazaki's Vision Of Japan's Most Iconic Monster Is Back Bigger Than Ever Battling An Even Deadlier Foe

The industry forecast on the high end was $30M+ in North America for Godzilla Minus Zero, which is boosted by Imax and a ton of PLFs, but now it could be way higher when you look at the comps that the sequel is beating.

Last year at this time, Disney kicked off November with Predator: Badlands with $4.8M in Wednesday and Thursday previews which yielded a $15.8M Friday and $40M opening. Godzilla Minus Zero in previews is very close to another U.S. Godzilla, Legendary’s 2019 Godzilla: King of the Monsters which posted $6.3M in Thursday previews on its way to a $19.4M Friday and $47.7M opening. Legendary has the rights from Toho to make any English-language version from the Godzilla universe. Godzilla Minus Zero is 91% certified fresh to Godzilla Minus One‘s 99% fresh with critics. That Oscar winning movie landed an A CinemaScore. The only other Godzilla to receive a solid A was Legendary’s Godzilla vs. Kong in 2021 (2024’s Godzilla x King roared an A-).

In Japan, Godzilla Minus Zero is up to 1.38 billion yen or $8.7M and that’s through three days. That opening Godzilla Day gross of 910M yen ($5.7M) is indeed a record for a live-action pic in Japan, besting the opening day of Harry Potter and the Chamber of Secrets (840M yen).

Warner Bros Pictures Animation’s net $86M production of The Cat in the Hat from filmmakers Alessandro Carloni and Erica Rivinoja is looking like $1M today after previews that began at 2PM. That’s on par with Sony Pictures Animation’s Goat which opened earlier this year and went on to open to $27.7M. Illumination/Universal’s The Grinch posted $2.2M in previews before a $67.5M opening weekend during the second frame of November 2018. Note, Cat in the Hat is one of the few family movies around this holiday season next to Disney’s girl-skewing Hexed which bows on the Wednesday before Thanksgiving.

Pay at Costco with a Visa, the only credit card its U.S. warehouses take. Get the Costco Anywhere Visa if you buy gas there, since it pays 5% on up to $7,000 a year. In the warehouse its 2% only matches a no-fee 2% card, and Robinhood’s Gold card beats it at 3% for a $50 yearly membership.

So the first question is whether you buy enough Costco gas to carry its card, and the second is which Visa should pay for your cart. Costco’s gas stations accept Visa and no other credit card as well, according to Costco’s membership terms, while Costco.com also takes Mastercard.

The store’s own cards come first, then flat-rate cards that pay the same rate on every purchase, then cards that pay more as your balance at their bank grows.

Both pay cash back once a year, after the February statement closes, as a certificate you redeem at a U.S. Costco warehouse for merchandise or cash.

At 5%, a household that buys $3,000 of gas a year at Costco’s pumps earns $150 with this card, against $60 on a 2% card.

Business owners who buy gas and supplies at Costco get the personal card’s rates on business spending, and each employee who holds a Costco membership can get a card at no fee.

With Robinhood’s $50-a-year Gold membership, it pays 3% in the warehouse and at the pump, and after that fee it beats a no-fee 2% card once you charge it more than $5,000 a year.

For more information, read our full Robinhood Gold Visa® Card Review.

Members who want one card with no annual fee and nothing else to join get 2% from it in the warehouse, at the pump, and everywhere else.

For more information, read our full Wells Fargo Active Cash® Card Review.

Fidelity investors earn 2% in the warehouse, as Costco’s own card does, but the cash back goes into a Fidelity brokerage, retirement, or college savings account instead of onto a Costco certificate.

U.S. Bank customers with $10,000 already in Smartly Checking, and a Smartly Savings account, earn 2.5% in the warehouse, and 4% at $100,000, on up to $10,000 of purchases a billing cycle.

For more information, read our full U.S. Bank Smartly™ Visa Signature® Card Review.

Bank of America checking customers in its BofA Rewards program earn 2.2% at wholesale clubs like Costco, or 3% once their deposit and investment balances there average $100,000, up to a quarterly cap.

For more information, read our full Bank of America® Customized Cash Rewards Credit Card Review.

At Costco, gas is where its own card leads, and the cart is where a flat rate or a bank balance decides what you earn. Put Costco gas on the Costco Anywhere Visa and pair it with a no-fee 2% Visa for the cart, or with the Robinhood Gold Card if you’d put over $5,000 a year on it.

Your Gut Sells Low and Buys High – Write Your Investing Rules Down First

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The credit card, banking, and other financial offers that appear on this site are from companies that compensate us – for example, when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. This compensation may influence how and where offers appear on the site, including the order in which they appear on category pages. It does not influence our evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We do not include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Your gut tells you to sell after a drop and buy after a run-up, the reverse of buying low and selling high. While you’re calm, write a one-page plan that sets your mix of stocks and bonds, monthly buys, rebalancing date, and reasons to sell. Then automate what you can and follow the plan, not your gut.

The S&P 500 index fell about a third from its Feb. 19, 2020, peak to its March 23 low, and investors who sold that day locked in the loss. By Aug. 18, the index had rallied more than 50% to a record close.

Selling felt safe that day because instinct is built to run from danger and chase what’s working. Four biases explain how it shows up in your trades.

Loss aversion makes a loss hurt more than an equal gain feels good, so a falling balance pushes you to sell just to stop the pain. Recency bias makes you expect the last few months to continue, so a slump feels endless and a hot streak feels safe. Herd behavior has you buy what everyone else is buying, usually after it has already run up. And after one good call, overconfidence makes you trust the next hunch more than it deserves.

The fix is to make each decision once, on a calm day, and write it down. Your one-page plan should cover four things:

Then automate as much of the plan as you can. A robo-advisor can manage your mix and rebalance it for you, which leaves you fewer choices to make during a market scare.

Finally, add a cooling-off rule. Before any trade your plan doesn’t call for, wait 48 hours and reread it.

To hand your mix and its rebalancing to software, compare the best robo-advisors.

Investing Isn’t Gambling – Hold Index Funds for Decades and Cap Bets at 5%

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The credit card, banking, and other financial offers that appear on this site are from companies that compensate us – for example, when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. This compensation may influence how and where offers appear on the site, including the order in which they appear on category pages. It does not influence our evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We do not include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Owning a diversified stock fund for years isn’t gambling. The companies in a fund earn profits that give it a positive average return, while casinos set up their games so players lose on average. Put your long-term money in the fund, and keep bets on next week’s price to a small slice you could lose entirely.

Judged one year at a time, the stock market can look like a casino. Large U.S. stocks, measured by the S&P 500 with dividends included, lost money in 26 of the 98 years from 1928 through 2025, according to year-by-year returns compiled by NYU Stern professor Aswath Damodaran.

Over all 98 years, though, they returned about 10% a year before inflation, because the companies behind them earn profits, pay part out as dividends, and reinvest the rest to grow. Holding longer is what makes the gain dependable. With dividends reinvested and before inflation, every 20-year stretch in the data, all 79 of them, ended with a gain. The worst, 1929 through 1948, still turned $1,000 into about $1,600.

At the roulette table, time works against you. On an American wheel, the house keeps about 5.26% of the money bet on red, according to Britannica, so a $1,000 bet there loses about $53 on average. The more spins you play, the closer your results get to losing 5.26% of everything you bet.

The line between investing and gambling is where the payoff comes from. Investing pays you from the profits that companies earn over years. A broad index fund owns hundreds of those companies at once by holding every stock in an index such as the S&P 500.

A bet on next week’s price or on a single hot stock is gambling, because it pays only if the price moves your way in time. So is buying options that expire in days, contracts that become worthless unless the stock passes a set price by then.

If you want to place those bets, use only money you could lose entirely, cap it at 5% of what you invest, and keep it in a separate account. Count any crypto you buy in that slice too.

Put everything else you invest in a broad index fund, and don’t sell it in the losing years, since every one of those 79 stretches had at least two.

Compare the best online brokers to pick where to buy that fund.

Helene Hahn Loyd, Former Disney and DreamWorks SKG Exec, Dies at 78

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Loyd died peacefully in her sleep on Oct. 24, in Park City, Utah.

Helene Hahn Loyd, the former Disney evp and one of the original executives at DreamWorks SKG, has died. She was 78.

Loyd died peacefully in her sleep on Oct. 24, in Park City, Utah.

She was born in Westbury, New York, and graduated from Hofstra University in 1970. After graduating, she moved to Los Angeles and began temporarily working at Paramount Pictures while attending law school. She subsequently earned her degree from Loyola Law School in 1975.

Loyd would rejoin Paramount Pictures in 1997 and became senior vice president. She would go on to work at Walt Disney Studios, being named executive vice president in 1981, elevating her among the highest level of women working in the film business. Working with Jeffrey Katzenberg, Loyd was a key player in Disney’s renaissance, playing a hand in the company’s partnership with Pixar. Related Stories TV Shari Rosenblum, Disney TV Studios' PR Lead, Exits the Company TV Kimmarie Johnson, 'Beverly Hills, 90210' Actress, Dies at 50

She was recruited as one of the first executives to build DreamWorks SKG in 1994, which was founded by Katzenberg, Steven Spielberg and David Geffen. There, Loyd worked her way up to chief operating officer. During the duration of her career, Loyd served as a mentor to many upcoming female executives.

Loyd left DreamWorks in 2004, moving to Park City and taking on skiing as a passion.

The executive’s husband of over two decades, Charles “Chuck” Loyd Jr., died in July.

The family has asked those who wish to make a donation in their memory to donate to U.S. Ski & Snowboard, honoring their shared love of skiing and their commitment to supporting the next generation of athletes.

Start with a no-fee business card that pays 2% on every purchase with no yearly cap, the best fit for most freelancers. Add a card with a higher rate on phone, internet, or gas only if those bills take a big share of your spending, since such cards pay 1% on everything else.

A business card puts your freelance costs on a statement of their own, so they’re easy to add up when you deduct them at tax time. Pay it from a business bank account to keep the business money apart from yours too. You can apply as a sole proprietor without forming an LLC first, and no card here charges an annual fee.

Choose by where your money goes. If it’s spread across many kinds of purchases, a card that pays the same rate on everything earns more, with no categories to track. Some freelancers put a big share of their spending into a few costs, like phone and internet, gas, or Amazon orders. Choose the American Express card built for your biggest spending. Households wanting cash back should get a Blue Cash card, while the Gold and Platinum pay off only if you’ll use their credits and travel with the points. Business owners should start with a no-fee Blue Business card.

The cards here pay you in cash back, in American Express’s Membership Rewards points, or in Delta miles and Hilton points. American Express’s own examples, figured on its Platinum Card, value a point at 1 cent toward flights booked through its Amex Travel site and 0.6 cent when you use it to pay a charge on your statement. So the points are worth most to people who travel. The Delta and Hilton cards serve regulars of those brands, and if you’re loyal to another airline or hotel chain, choose among all airline credit cards or hotel credit cards instead.

The Gold and Platinum come with statement credits, money back on your bill, but most arrive as monthly, quarterly, or twice-yearly amounts you collect only by making the purchases they name in that period. Disney TV Reorg Chatter Heats Up As Next Wave Of Layoffs Draws Near – The Dish

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The long-awaited restructuring of Disney Entertainment Television under new Chairman Debra OConnell is starting to get real. As reported on Monday, Disney has initiated the next round of company-wide layoffs by handing managers targets for the size of the cuts they need to make, with some asked to achieve as much as 20% in annual cost savings.

That round, whose timing looks like early 2027, likely January, is expected to be intertwined with OConnell’s reorg. Areas seriously impacted by layoffs are believed to include the TV networks division of Disney Entertainment Television, which already has been the subject of major cuts over the past couple of years as Disney is moving to decrease its dependance on the declining linear TV business. (The company recently sold its 50% stake in A+E Global Media to Hearst for $1.2B.) Related Stories News Lionsgate & 3 Arts Set New Horror Banner Render Road Led By Luke Maxwell & Will Rowbotham - The Dish News 'Toy Story 5' Writer-Director Andrew Stanton Wants To Bring Nuance To The Conversation Between Kids & Technology

Nothing is imminent, and OConnell has kept her plans close to the vest, leaving Hollywood guessing about what the new structure would look like. That has not stopped people from speculating about potential moves. Watch on Deadline

There has been chatter about further consolidation of 20th Television and ABC/Hulu.

In her first major executive move after being named Chairman of Disney Entertainment in March, OConnell added 20th Television to the purview of Disney Television Group President Craig Erwich, who already had been overseeing ABC Entrainment and Hulu Originals, with 20th Television President Karey Burke reporting to him.

With 20th Television increasingly focused on supplying Disney platforms, integrating the two areas would not be unreasonable. (The studio, whose predecessor 20th Century Fox Television was a leading seller to everyone, has a small third-party business with such series as CBS’ Tracker, Netflix’s Nobody Wants This and The Beast In Me and Apple TV’s Imperfect Women.)

There had been speculation about Burke potentially expanding her turf and what the future holds for Erwich and his top lieutenant Simran Sethi, President, Scripted Programming, Hulu Originals and ABC Entertainment. Despite the background noise, especially around Sethi, it’s been business as usual for the duo who have been evaluating scripts and making decisions.

Industry insiders point out that, after absorbing Touchstone Television and then ABC Signature, 20th Television’s senior executive ranks under Burke had naturally expanded over the years, so streamlining the team via layoffs may be in the cards.

Besides FX Networks, which sits outside of OConnell’s domain, with Chairman John Landgraf reporting to Disney President and Chief Creative Officer Dana Walden, the Disney cable networks have been battered by deep cuts. While Freeform was folded into Erwich’s and by extension Sethi’s portfolio awhile back, National Geographic Content, led by President Courteney Monroe, has remained a separate unit reporting to OConnell. It will be interesting to see if that will remain the case in the new structure if Nat Geo is gutted by layoffs.

Another area drawing speculation is getting content teams working on Hulu and Disney+ programming integrated in some way, with someone that has expertise in both, like former Fox executive Charlie Andrews, currently EVP at Disney+/Disney Channel-focused Disney Branded Television, potentially getting a bigger role.

One piece of OConnell’s structure was recently revealed when Joe Earley, previously Co-President, Direct-To-Consumer for Disney Entertainment, reporting to Walden, moved to OConnell’s Disney Entertainment Television group to take on the role of President, Disney Entertainment Television Franchise and Content Strategy.

However the reorg shakes out, the cuts across Disney Entertainment Television are expected to be substantial, alleviated in part by Disney’s voluntary early retirement offers that I hear more executives than expected took across the entire company.

As for the timing of OConnell’s restructuring, while it feels like it’s been taking forever, if it comes in January as rumored, that would be about 10 months after OConnell was named Chairman of Disney Entertainment Television. That is not out of norm: Jinny Howe, Netflix‘s Head of UCAN (U.S.-Canada) Scripted Series, just announced the structure of her division almost 14 months after she took over.

Europa League: AC Milan, the ugly relapse

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Five days after dominating Inter in Serie A in the most popular derby in Lombardy (3-1), AC Milan fell again this Thursday in the Europa League, beaten at San Siro by Ferencvaros.

The Budapest club, although reduced to ten after the expulsion of Dele in the 77e minute, won in Italy with a score of 0-2 and a double from Joseph in the second half (52e66e).

With three defeats on the clock in four days, the Rossoneri, already previously dominated by Benfica and Bournemouth, fall to the last part of the table in the general classification, thus virtually eliminated halfway through the league phase.

On the other hand, the Hungarian club confirms its appetite for Europe and joins the Bournemouth Cherries in control of the Europa League with a full 12 points.

Manu Tournoux is a passionate and knowledgeable football enthusiast with a special focus on French football. 📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Kourtney Kardashian set the record straight about her marriage to husband Travis Barker after she was asked to comment on a rumor that they "don’t live together anymore."

Kourtney Kardashian is keeping chatter at bay.

The Kardashians star called out "fabricated stories" about her marriage to husband Travis Barker, denying there's trouble in paradise after her publicist asked her to comment on breakup speculation.

"She is like, ‘The magazine is saying that you and Travis don’t live together anymore and you are splitting up,'" Kourtney shared on the Nov. 5 episode of her Hulu series. "I go, ‘Tell them to print it.’ Just see what happens. They know it is not true."

In fact, the 47-year-old believes the rumor was started by some "dumb" TikTok video.

"And people believe it!" she bemoaned. "It's so bizarre."

And the breakup rumor is far from the truth. After all, Kourtney and Travis—who share 3-year-old son Rocky Barker—have been renovating their home so that there's enough room for their family, which also includes kids Mason Disick, 16, Penelope Disick, 14, Reign Disick, 11, with her ex Scott Disick as well as Atiana de la Hoya, 27, Landon Barker, 23, and Alabama Barker, 20, from the Blink-182 drummer's past marriage to Shanna Moakler.

"I feel like blending, in the beginning, is a lot of adjusting for each person," she continued. "Us in their space and then us being out of our space and into a new space."

And though Kourtney admittedly feels "vulnerable" being temporarily displaced from her home due to construction, she's pooshing for new family memories.

"I can't wait to cook, have movie nights, barbecues," Kourtney raved while touring the home on the Nov. 5 episode. "I want to make it feel magical for everyone."

In the series, Nora embarks on a deeply personal cross-country journey as she explores contemporary Asian American cuisine, starting with her family’s remarkable legacy. Nora’s family ran Lum’s, the iconic Cantonese restaurant in Flushing, New York, and the very first in a now thriving Chinatown. But despite growing up in the family business, she can’t cook. At all. Optimism new proposal weakens council power over standalone chains

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The proposed policy would let qualifying transfers proceed without another governance vote, changing who users rely on for upgrades.

An Optimism governance proposal published Nov. 5 would require at least 30 days' public notice before a chain transfers upgrade control from the shared Security Council, giving users time to assess changes to the network's security model.

The proposed Standard Rollup Charter amendment concerns chains whose upgrade authority rests with the Council under the Charter. It would let a Chain Governor request that control move to an address of its choosing, so it could manage upgrades itself or through a third party.

A completed transfer would mean relying on the security model of the new upgrade key holder. Optimism Governance and the Security Council would no longer retain the same practical ability to enforce the Charter's policies on that chain through upgrade authority.

The policy's accompanying GitHub amendment PR 74 was still open on Nov. 5 and says it will merge only if governance accepts the proposal.

A governor would first submit a request to the Optimism Foundation, which would confirm that it is valid and complies with the governor's commitments. The Foundation would then publish notice on the Governance Forum.

That notice should identify the chain, its Chain ID, and material layer-1 contract addresses. It should also warn that the chain would cease to be a Standard Rollup if the transfer goes ahead.

The Security Council would have to wait at least 30 days from publication before executing the transaction. The proposed policy says the Council should check that the Foundation published the notice, that the minimum period has elapsed, and that the recipient address matches the one the Foundation supplied on the governor's behalf.

A transfer meeting the approved policy's conditions would need no additional Optimism Governance approval. That would remove the need to seek a separate vote for each handover.

Before transfer, the Council would retain upgrade authority under the Charter. A qualifying transfer would need no further governance vote.

Under the proposed route, users would rely on the chosen key holder. A qualifying transfer would need no further governance vote.

A completed transfer would change upgrade control and end Standard Rollup status.

Proposed policy as of November 5, 2026. Chain agreements remain; no executed transfer or universal user exit guarantee is established.

Maps the proposed sequence and before/after authority relationship from the official proposal. The interval denotes a minimum of 30 days after notice; other spacing is schematic, not a duration.

Proposed policy as of November 5, 2026. Chain agreements remain; no executed transfer or universal user exit guarantee is established.

The proposal preserves agreements with the Foundation, including legal agreements and restrictions on when a governor may request a transfer.

A chain would lose Standard Rollup status when it executes a transfer, and the Foundation would update its status in the Superchain Registry. External assessments of the chain's decentralization stage could also change.

The notice window would give users time to assess who would control future upgrades and whether to keep using the chain under that arrangement.

The next milestone is governance acceptance followed by merger of the Charter amendment. Adopting the policy would require no changes to smart contracts or node software, and any future handover would begin with a separate request and public notice.

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Hyperliquid Strategies Inc., the largest publicly traded HYPE treasury company, announced that its subsidiary has applied for registration as a US futures commission merchant (FCM).

We deployed $802 million this quarter and filed an application to become a futures commission merchant. Those two moves are the same bet.

Hyperliquid was dominant in the part of on-chain finance that delivers real fees, and this value flows back to HYPE holders through systematic buybacks, the CEO added.

The company reported $620.6 million in quarterly profits. However, $803 million in unrealized gains came from its HYPE holdings, which totaled 38.9 million, with an average cost of $56.35 per token.

It raised $911 million in equity capital for the quarter ending on the 30th of September, spending $785 million to acquire 9.1 million HYPE tokens.

An FCM is a registered intermediary that holds customer funds and handles futures orders. The subsidiary would serve permissioned markets under HIP-3*, a planned upgrade whose regulatory approvals are pending.

If approved, Hyperliquid would get a regulated entry point for U.S. traders in the same week. There is no guaranteed timeline for the HIP-3*’s approval, the company stated.

Right now, the company’s stock, PURR, is highly correlated to HYPE token price swings.

On the 29th of October, billionaire American investor Stan Druckenmiller tweeted about Hyperliquid. Santiment Intelligence reported that this pushed the token price to local highs of $97.

Whale wallets were steadily selling their holdings during the rally. Four whales closed their longs entirely, banking around $5 million. The largest of these dumped $225k every five minutes when the price was around $95 and short-term sentiment was euphoric.

The weekly bullish structure extended from $51.13 to $97.99 (orange). The daily structure was also bullish, but the $75.17 swing low from September has been challenged once already.

The recent retreat from $97 revealed the sellers’ strength. A deeper drawdown below $75.17 remains likely. In that case, swing traders and investors should watch the $60.50-$68.35 demand zone.

How Zara Larsson’s MUA Makes Pink Eyeshadow Chic, Not Costumey

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Think pink eyeshadow isn't wearable? Zara Larsson's makeup artist Ernesto Casillas taught us exactly how you can pull off the bold hue.

Zara Larsson has proven that her influence as a pop girlie is never ending—and that all comes down to her bubbly energy and playful fashion choices.

The Swedish singer's Y2K-inspired stage looks consisting of bold colors and light-catching shimmer aren't complete without her equally fun makeup looks.

While she's not one to shy away from color, at the Billboard Women in Music event, Zara's makeup artist Ernesto Casillas wanted to create a "softer, feminine, but...still somewhat colorful," eye look that could transition from the red carpet to the stage.

Casillas exclusively shared with E! how he created Zara's "Malibu Barbie" eye look.

If pink eyeshadow feels too punchy for regular wear, Casillas told E! exactly how he makes it look chic rather than costumey.

"I had fun by using pinks, just more wearable pinks," he said. Think "frosted champagne pinks on the lid, maybe a little blush on the crease."

Another key is to craft your preferred shape using more neutral hues. Casillas suggests "A pinky brown eyeshadow through the crease, just to softly contour it. But I wanted it to be a little more pink. So I dipped into a little blush, and softly went over the crease, just to warm it up even more."

This affordable three-pan palette has everything you need to recreate Zara's look, including a warm brown crease color, soft pink lid shade, and shimmery topper.

For more variety, you can opt for this 12-pan palette packed with highly-pigmented shimmers and metallics in addition to blendable matte transition shades.

To add a little extra punch, Ernesto suggests lightly dipping a shadow brush into a bold pink blush to deepen the crease. This monochromatic trio includes a satin and velvet powder plus a luminous balm. Apply it to your cheeks for a cohesive look.

"I knew she would be on stage, so I knew to play up, glossy and frosty textures, because the light will pick up all that beautiful shine on the eyelids, on the cheeks, and on the lips," shared Casillas.

To amplify Zara's subtle pink eyeshadow, he made sure to add shimmer at the center of the lids, along the cheekbones, paired with a glossy lip.

Intensify a soft pink eye look by adding a shimmery topper to the center and inner corner of your eyes. This one from Half Magic offers an intense color payoff in just a few swipes.

To bring the whole look together, Casillas added a few lash clusters from Lashify to define Zara's cat-like eye shape.

"I placed [the Stardust style] on the outside of her eye, because she likes a very feline eye shape. So I did that to accentuate her shape," he said. "Then I did the spike, [using] the Cherry Stax across the entire eye to give her a dolly look."

Lashify's individual clusters allow you to customize your perfect fake lash. Available in several colors and lengths, concentrate them on the outer ends of the eye to produce an elongated effect.

Pair Lashify's clusters with their powerful long-hold adhesive with a barely there feel.

Fenty Beauty's iconic gloss bomb in the bestselling shade FU$$Y is the perfect finishing touch to your Zara-inspired look. It delivers a “your lips but better” wash with a hint of light-catching shimmer.

EXCLUSIVE: Ryan Serhant Warns Homebuyers Against Waiting for 2027: ‘You Don’t Want to Wait Until It’s Too Late’

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Ryan Serhant is warning would-be homebuyers against assuming that lower mortgage rates will deliver an easier path to homeownership in 2027—and says the housing market has a problem that cheaper borrowing alone cannot solve.

Speaking exclusively to Realtor.com®, Serhant shared his outlook for the year ahead, identified markets he believes could surprise buyers, and weighed in on the debate over private listings.

He also offered a glimpse inside his own townhouse and revealed a buyer request that has him searching for an enormous stretch of land with its own airport.

At the center of his housing outlook is a concern about people at opposite ends of the property ladder being unable to move.

“We have entered an environment where people can't afford to buy their first home, and their parents can't afford to leave their last one, and it's a problem we need to solve," he said.

“Lower mortgage rates will help, but rates alone are not going to fix the cost of housing or the cost of moving. We need to create more supply and fewer obstacles," Serhant added.

Serhant argued that the system has become an obstacle to moving, with consequences that have developed over many years.

“I think that what we've created is a environment, that is punishing mobility. Instead of rewarding mobility. And this was set up a long, long time ago. We didn't get here overnight.”

“Like, the housing market and the system we operate in rewards stability, because what's… it makes everyone feel comfortable. But then when that stability does not move, because stability is stability, mobility is mobility. Then everyone gets angry, and that's the environment that we're currently in.”

For his outlook, he is watching whether households have both the confidence and the resources to act.

“I think I'm watching employment, and I'm watching household confidence right now. I think pent-up demand only becomes a transaction when people have the confidence and the financial ability to act, and we'll see if that happens anytime soon.”

For people who can afford to buy but are considering waiting for a better deal in 2027, Serhant pointed to opportunities he believes already exist.

“I think in a lot of markets, we've entered a buyer's market now. You have the ability to negotiate in markets that you haven't been able to negotiate in in the past couple years.”

“I think there's a great opportunity to use the rate pressure that we have to negotiate closing costs, to negotiate rate buy-downs, to negotiate lower purchase prices, to get a house that really, really, really works for you. Remember, it is such an old and annoying saying, but you do marry the house, and you do date through the rates.”

His argument rests on the potential trade-off between borrowing costs and competition, although his comments about future rates and inventory are predictions.

“The market does become very stagnant. Interest rates should come down, and remember, you can refinance. And you should use the opportunity right now, if you can afford it, to purchase in a high-interest rate environment, because there's less competition.”

“Everyone wants low rates, they never want what comes with it. You know what'll happen if rates come down? There will be no houses for sale.”

“You won't even have the opportunity to complain about a high monthly payment. You won't even be able to talk about—the lack of affordability. You won't be able to complain, because there will be nothing for sale, because the demand is so great. There is not nearly enough houses for the demand we have in this country. Every 40-year-old who still lives at home will then move.”

Serhant also argued that the circumstances accompanying lower rates can make buyers reluctant to purchase.

“And what also brings down low interest rates is job insecurity. You know, is unemployment, is war. Are things that happen in the global environment that will make people, not confident to go and make a massive purchase. And so, you might miss that opportunity, too.”

He used the early months of the pandemic to illustrate his point about confidence and timing.

“In the spring of 2020, that was a great time to buy. But no, we had COVID, 'You can't buy. There's a death ticker on the bottom of every TV screen. You might not live.' You didn't take advantage of this environment, and then when the market came back, it was too late.”

“And so, I would use this moment to move quickly, because when things do even out, and they always do, markets go up and markets go down. Always. And forever. You don't want to wait until it's too late.”

Serhant highlighted returning city residents and investment in jobs as themes behind his market picks.

“I think people are returning to cities in a way that we haven't seen before. It's one of the reasons we just were launching, officially Chicago. This coming Wednesday. I'm incredibly excited about that.”

“The demand and the volume we've seen from all of our other Serhant markets wanting to move back to Chicago, who moved out over the past couple years. Taking advantage of good value, like we just talked about, is really exciting.”

“And I think places where you're seeing incredible job growth. Areas like Ohio, where there's major, major tech investment, not just in data centers. In office. And in manufacturing.”

“We have a lot of investment that's going into Western Pennsylvania, and, you're also seeing a lot of movement into Alabama.”

“You know, I think there are markets that will really, really surprise you because of great quality of life for the best cost structure the country has to offer.”

Serhant also addressed private listings, beginning with his explanation of how they differ from properties marketed publicly.

“A private listing is the opposite of a public to the consumer listing," he said.

“It has been the standard over the past… 20-some-odd years—that if you want to market your home, so that there is a fair and free market and competitive marketplace, that if you opt to work with a real estate agent. That you have to bring your home to the public marketplace.”

“Obviously, every home that's not on the market is technically a private listing. The Empire State Building is a private listing, for what it's worth. The whole world is a private listing.”

He then offered his criticism of listing restrictions and penalties. The following is his characterization of the debate, rather than a description of a single policy applying to every platform or multiple listing service.

“But what has changed now is the MLSs, and the listing aggregators have installed fees and penalties.”

“So, if you list with them, as you're supposed to, you can have the prize of being public, but if you don't list with them, because you want to work with an agent, but you don't want your home to be blasted all over the world, then you can never list with them ever, and we will also punish you, potentially with fines.”

“The current debate is over, should that be allowed? Who actually owns the data, and who actually owns the property? And access to it. Is it the brokerage? Is it the websites that showcase listings? Is it the multiple listing services? Is it the owner of the property?”

For Serhant, the discussion should focus on what sellers receive from the process.

“And that's what the debate is over right now, is what they say, consumer choice. I think what is broken is that it's not about consumer choice. I think everyone's focused on the wrong thing. I think people should be focused on consumer value.”

“Where's the upside? Where's the consumer upside? You know, the battle right now is over commission fees, and who gets a piece. And I think that's gross, and I'm in this business.”

“My position is pretty simple. I think your home deserves the whole market if you'd like it to. And if a seller genuinely wants privacy, we need to respect that. But then you have to explain the trade-off. Privacy and maximum exposure are different objectives.”

On concerns that private listings could reduce a seller's pool of buyers or disadvantage people without the right connections, Serhant acknowledged the risks.

“I think there are real concerns. But I think everybody has to be fully aware of what those concerns are. And again, I think it should be up to the consumer, and unfortunately, these rules exist because in a world where there is no rules, and there is no punishment. Not everyone is a good actor.”

He drew on his own experience working as an agent during the financial crisis.

“Like, all the people that are leading this fight, none of them were real estate agents like I was in 2008 and 2009. None of them were trying to get business on Craigslist. None of them were trying to figure out what was for sale and what wasn't for sale.”

“None of them were trying to figure out if the listing agent in front of them was lying to their client or not. None of them were trying to figure out, with the fiduciary responsibility of a buyer in front of you, how to give them the most accurate and up-to-date information.”

“None of them were, like me, on the streets of New York City, or trying to do deals in Florida and California, trying to figure out what is actually for sale, and if a buyer isn't paying an excise commission by working with that agent, they actually won't even get that property? Is that really? What we want to go back to?”

“I feel like a homeowner's value and a homeowner's biggest asset shouldn't become a real estate brokerage's, or an MLS's, or a listing aggregator's recruiting tool.”

Away from the business of buying and selling, Serhant shared the first thing he does when he walks into his townhouse.

“I yell out my daughter's name. I wanted to see where she is in my house. We have a townhouse, so I'm trying to figure out which floor she's on, so I can, you know, prepare.”

His favorite spaces include a gym on the lower level, although his affection extends to the whole property.

“I have a pretty awesome gym. In the lower level that I built out, that's really, really cool, and I think that's pretty awesome, but I mean, I built it with my bare hand. It was a TV show that we did for Bravo about our renovation, and I love that house. Whole thing is great.”

He also described the practical details that help make his kitchen work, starting with a concealed ladder for reaching high cabinets.

“We have incredibly high ceilings. So we have a hidden ladder in our kitchen that looks like the baseboard under the kitchen. Island, and it pops out with the touch of your toe.”

“And, the ladder shows up, and so for anyone, you know, you know, who's vertically challenged to be able to reach upper cabinetry without having a ladder in front of your face at all times, so that you can still enjoy all that light and sunshine and high ceilings, I think is key.”

“Probably the greatest things that we have also in that kitchen are touchless, so, like, the sink, you know, you're cooking, and your hands are messy, and you're always hitting the thing. We have the kitchen sink, where all you have to do is tap it, and it turns on.”

“And so you just hit it with the back of your hand, and it turns on. So you're not, like, also getting the sink and the faucet dirty.”

“And then the same thing for the kitchen refrigerator and freezer. All you do is you slowly kind of, like, tap it with your elbow, or with the side, so you're not, like, yanking on anything, and it automatically just reveals itself to you. I think those little touches are overlooked.”

Among the unusual requests Serhant receives, one current search involves a vast amount of land—and a private airport.

“They're all wild. I'm dealing with somebody right now that wants 100,000 acres of land, which is impossible, for the most part."

For all the features a buyer might want, Serhant has one clear priority when choosing a home.

“You can change everything about the house you want over the years, you can grow into it, you can grow out of it, you can update it, you can paint it, you can flip it upside down. Unless you are Ken Griffin, and you can pick up a house, and you can move it, for the most part. Change the location.”

“And so, you always buy location first. Whatever that is, wherever that place is you want to be for the next 5-10 years, and most times you're paying more. But no one ever paid too much. And regretted it years later. Very, very rarely does that happen.”

“Remember, if you buy a trophy, you get to sell a trophy. If you buy a discount, because you're cheap, you will have to sell a discount. And you will feel cheap.”

Kelsi Karruli is a senior celebrity reporter who joined Realtor.com in 2025. She writes about trends encompassing real estate and celebrity properties. She previously wrote for Daily Mail, True Urban Culture, and JMedia Corporation, where she specialized in covering celebrities, relationships, psychology, and gender issues. She has a bachelor's degree in journalism from Pace University, where she received the James Rose Award for excellence in journalism. When she isn’t updating readers on A-listers' moves, she is spending time with her cat, Mary, and cheering on the Albanian soccer team.

Apple explains how to turn off the new MacBook Pro’s glowing logo

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

While the illuminated Apple logo on the new MacBook Pro drew one of the biggest cheers during last week’s announcement, Apple knows that not everyone will want to keep it on. Here’s the company’s official guide to turning it off.

The new MacBook Pro with PROLED XDR Display marks the return of the illuminated Apple logo on its lid after a decade.

As Chance Miller explained in his review of the new MacBook Pro, “unlike older MacBooks, which used light from the display’s backlight to illuminate the logo, the new OLED MacBook Pro has no backlight to draw from, requiring a different approach.”

He added that, as with the old Macs, the logo’s brightness is tied to the screen brightness, though it also accounts for overall ambient brightness. He also noted that “when the MacBook Pro is closed, the logo looks like polished metal, just like it has since the 2016 redesign.”

The review echoed some of the findings developer Gui Rambo shared on X a few days ago:

The glowing Apple logo on the new MacBook Pro is controllable by software. It has 25kHz PWM and per-device calibration. CoreBrightness controls the relationship between screen and logo brightness. The brightness of the logo is adjusted differently depending on the enclosure color pic.twitter.com/baqBEkzJUo

Now, Apple has published an official support document that also explains how the illuminated logo works and how to turn it off completely.

The Apple logo behind the built-in display is illuminated whenever the Mac is awake. It automatically dims or brightens based on the brightness level of the built-in screen and the lighting conditions detected by the computer’s ambient light sensor. For all screen-brightness levels, the Apple logo will be dimmer in a dim room and brighter in a bright room.

For users who’d rather keep the logo turned off at all times, Apple explains that they can run the following command in Terminal, provided they have the administrator password:

sudo defaults write com.apple.CoreBrightness LEBacklightMode -int 0

To revert the command and have the logo be illuminated again, users will need to run the following command:

sudo defaults delete com.apple.CoreBrightness LEBacklightMode

Apple notes that reinstalling macOS will also turn the logo illumination back on.

To read Apple’s full support article, follow this link. And make sure to also catch 9to5Mac’s review of the new MacBook Pro.

For Natural Volume, Wash Your Hair The French Girl Way

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

We all know that French girls have that special je ne sais quoi (so much so that it’s inspired countless essays on this very site). When it comes to their hair, the style is nonchalant—and yet, somehow also skillfully cultivated. It’s hair that’s air-dries, falls just right, and retains that slightly tousled movement that gives no hint of effort or styling. So how exactly do Jeanne Damas, Brigitte Bardot, Jane Birkin, and more do it?

Hairstylist and French girl herself Daphné Narcy has an idea: It all starts how you wash your hair. “You may be damaging your hair without even realizing it while in the shower,” Narcy says. “When you’re hair is wet, it’s at its most fragile. But habits like washing hair too quickly, scrubbing too hard, or using water that’s too hot may be causing breakage and frizz.”

She offers up eight simple shower-time adjustments to turn your hair into a French girl dream.

The first—and by far the most common—mistake: People wash their hair instead of their scalp. “We rub the lengths against each other, which damages them, and we neglect the roots, where sebum, pollution, and residue accumulate,” says Narcy. Her philosophy can be summed up in one sentence: A healthy scalp is the foundation of healthy hair. It’s also what determines how light the roots feel once the hair is dry.

The heat from a scalding shower lifts the hair cuticle, which can be bad for shine. “The hair loses moisture, becomes more porous and duller, and the color fades faster,” Narcy says. Hot water hurts the scalp, too, making it drier and more irritated. “Water that’s too hot also softens the hair follicles, which can lead to hair loss.” Your new rule of thumb here is disarmingly simple: “If the water feels pleasant on your wrist, without feeling hot, it’s the right temperature for your hair.”

Use your fingertips in small circular motions, without ever scrubbing. “It’s meant to be an invigorating massage, not a scrub,” insists Narcy. It’s also a moment of pleasantness. When you enjoy it, washing your hair becomes a ritual rather than a chore.

If you’ve ever read the back of a shampoo bottle, you know the instructions: Lather, rinse, repeat. And Narcy says that isn’t just to get you to use more product; but instead the double-shampoo is key to keeping hair clean—though only use a walnut-sized amount each time.

“When you use too much shampoo, residue can remain on the scalp, weighing it down, potentially irritating it, and making your hair look dirty faster The result: you end up washing your hair more often, and you get caught in a vicious cycle.”

The lengths should only be lightly touched when it comes to shampooing. “Press your hands together and slide them down, using very light and gentle friction, all the way to the ends. While rinsing, the lather that runs down finishes cleansing the lengths.” Less friction means fewer tangles, less breakage, and mid-lengths that not only stay hydrated but also dry smooth.

After EA, Silver Lake and PIF Reportedly Want Unity

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Contributor at DualShockers, former Esports Insider DACH content lead, former Escapist Magazine Germany content lead, software engineering background.

Unity Software has held talks to be acquired by a consortium that includes private equity firm Silver Lake and Saudi Arabia’s Public Investment Fund, according to Reuters. The group discussed a valuation of around $25 billion, whilst Unity’s market cap sits at roughly $21.5 billion after its latest earnings report. Shares rose about 4% in extended trading once the story broke.

Nothing is certain. The approach came in recent weeks, and Unity has reportedly been working with an investment bank to weigh its options ever since. Reuters notes that the plan and terms could still change, and Unity declined to comment.

Silver Lake and PIF have done this before. Both were part of the consortium behind the $55 billion EA take-private that closed in August, which ranks among the largest leveraged buyouts ever. A Unity deal would be their second enormous gaming bet in a matter of months.

Silver Lake isn’t walking in cold, either. It invested up to $400 million in Unity back in 2017 and still owns around 8% of the company. Its Egon Durban sits on Unity’s board, a dual role that would normally see him recuse himself from any board discussion of a sale. That’s the kind of detail that looks tidy on paper and gets picked at later.

Most people know Unity as the engine behind thousands of mobile, console and PC games, but its reach goes further than that. It’s used in automotive design, filmmaking, architecture and industrial simulation too. The company started out in Denmark in 2004, went public in September 2020, and has since wandered into advertising technology.

That advertising side is currently pulling its weight. Unity’s third quarter beat estimates, with revenue up 32% to $622.1 million and adjusted earnings of 44 cents per share. Strong growth in its AI-powered Vector platform helped it post its first quarterly profit on a GAAP basis since the IPO, alongside a solid showing from its game-development business. The takeover report landed straight afterwards. A company that has just turned its first profit is a much easier sell to a buyer, and I doubt that’s a coincidence.

There’s a practical angle too. According to Reuters, Unity’s tools are already used by Scopely, the developer behind Monopoly Go!, and Niantic, the creator of Pokémon Go, both of which are owned by a group that includes PIF, Silver Lake and Affinity Partners. The buyers would be acquiring something their own studios already rely on.

PIF’s route into gaming mostly runs through Savvy Games Group, which has had a mandate to invest $38 billion in the sector since 2022. Savvy bought Scopely for $4.9 billion in 2023, and Scopely then bought Niantic’s games business, Pokémon Go included, for $3.5 billion. ESL and FACEIT sit under the same umbrella. In January, PIF moved around $12 billion in listed gaming shares across to Savvy, including its stakes in Nintendo and Bandai Namco, followed by its roughly $3 billion Take-Two stake a month later. Add the EA deal on top and the list of major publishers with no Saudi money attached gets shorter every year.

The group discussed a valuation of around $25 billion, whilst Unity’s market cap sits at roughly $21.5 billion after its latest earnings report.

Savvy’s chief, Brian Ward, said at GDC that it doesn’t plan to use those stakes to push publishers into big changes. Taking him at his word, a minority shareholder is one thing. Owning the engine that other studios build on, including studios that compete with your own, is a different kind of position entirely.

Unity has been here before with developer trust. In September 2023 it announced a Runtime Fee that charged studios per install, and the backlash was fierce enough that CEO John Riccitiello stepped down within weeks. The fee was scrapped completely in September 2024, and current CEO Matthew Bromberg later admitted Unity had been “practically at war with our customers”. A leveraged buyout means debt, and debt has to be paid from somewhere. Developers are going to want to know where.

Right now, none of that is more than speculation. There are talks, an investment bank, and a number somewhere north of where the stock was trading. Studios will be watching to see which of those three moves first.

You Need A New Car? Go Electric! It’s Superior Technology! Part 1

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Your car is getting “long in tooth,” or maybe your daughter is going away to college and you are giving her your car. Whatever the reason, you are looking for another car. It could be new, it could be used: don’t even look at gas cars, electric cars are superior technology!

The wheels on every railroad locomotive in the world are driven by electric motors. All the busiest rail routes in Europe and China are pure electric, including high-speed rail. 98% of new cars in Norway are electric, over 80% of new cars in Denmark are electric. In China, the country with the largest sales of new cars in the world, the percentage of new electric cars in September 2026 was 67%. 82% of the operating bus fleet in China is electric. Even ships and airplanes are going electric. The data is unequivocal: electric propulsion is superior to internal combustion propulsion. Only the huge political contributions to Trump by the fossil fuel industry and his resulting anti-EV policies have slowed down adoption in this country.

In places we’ve been like California; Portland, Oregon; and northern Utah, you are seeing a lot of Teslas, so you know that electric cars are working for a lot of people these days. It’s time for you to do some research on electric cars.

You do pay a premium for new electric cars, because the batteries are still quite expensive. If price is the driving factor for you, buy a used electric car. Hardly any used EVs are high mileage, and EV batteries still have plenty of life even after they go off warranty at 8 years and 120,000 miles. There are thousands of lightly used EVs sitting at used car lots at reasonable prices all over the country these days. Some used car dealers will give you an estimate of the battery life. If not, get a friend who is an experienced EV driver to help you evaluate the battery life of the EV you are considering.

If you just do local driving or have two cars and have a garage or off-street parking, an EV is a no brainer. EVs are hands-down more convenient than gas cars for local driving. Your EV will have plenty of range for local driving, it will get a complete charge overnight in your home garage refueling station and it will never need an oil change.

EVs are as quiet as mice, as smooth as kittens, and as quick as jackrabbits. They have simple electric motors, not complex internal combustion engines. They have simple one-speed transmissions, not complex 7-speed automatic transmissions. They don’t have exhaust systems with mufflers that will rust and need replacement. They don’t have emission control systems with limited life. They don’t have complex cooling systems with big radiators. You seldom use your brakes with an EV, so your brakes may last the life of the car. They don’t have a big lead-acid battery for starting, with a maximum life of 5 years. New EVs have only a small lithium-ion battery, which lasts 10+ years. Bottom line: Routine maintenance for EVs is almost nil. I think the only real maintenance item on a Tesla over the first 3 years is the cabin air filter. You can expect an EV to go 500,000 miles without major repair or replacement in most cases.

Perhaps you can stomach buying a car from Tesla whose CEO has become one of the most despicable persons on the planet lately. (Example: Musk recently suspended access to his Starlink communications system to the Ukrainians who are valiantly defending their country from the Russian aggressors.) However, Tesla has sold almost 10 million automobiles worldwide that don’t emit CO2 and particulate pollution while operating. Tesla also has by far the most ubiquitous charging system, which allows cross-country driving on major US roads with little planning. Consider buying a Tesla.

So, what about cross-country trips? Everyone knows that long-distance trips in an electric car are inconvenient or even impossible, because there aren’t enough charging stations and it takes an hour if you can find one. Wrong: Every 100 miles along all the major Interstate highways, Tesla has built Superchargers with 8 to 32 and a few with over 100 charging stalls. By the time you have gone to the bathroom, walked the dog, and chatted with a fellow EV owner for a few minutes, you probably have more than enough charge to continue your journey. Usually, charging takes about 20 minutes. If you need a longer charge, you may need to watch a bit of Octopus Teacher or Back to the Future II on the big screen in the car for another 10 minutes.

News flash! Since February 28, 2023, most Tesla Superchargers (now North American Charging Standard/NACS chargers) are usable by all EV brands. Unfortunately, older Tesla Superchargers can’t be used by other brands, so I would still advise buying a Tesla if you want carefree cross-country driving. CCS chargers which are native to most other brands have sprung up by the thousands in the US, which together with the newer Tesla Superchargers make it possible to travel cross-country with non-Teslas on many routes with some planning. Also, Teslas can use non-Tesla chargers. Non-Tesla chargers often come with Tesla cables now, so Tesla owners don’t even need an adapter in most cases. However, even Teslas require careful planning if you are driving far from major highways.

My wife and I have done cross-country trips from Utah to Wisconsin for 7 years, on to North Carolina (once), and back to Utah. We have also traveled from Utah to Southern California at the same 500 miles per day rate that we used to do with our gas cars … and with no worries, mate.

I’m going to try to pass on some things I’ve learned from driving five different electric vehicles over 12 years and some 200,000 miles. See part two, coming soon.

If you find any of my articles helpful to you, please use my referral link when buying a new Tesla: https://ts.la/arthur73734 (Be sure to use it when you make your order). Tesla keeps changing the program, but here’s the latest: If you are buying a new Tesla Model 3 or Y and use my link, you will get two months of free Full Self Driving (a ~$200 value) on top of the one month that comes free with every new Model Y right now. This will get you a total of 3 months free Full Self Driving. It will be $99/month after that. For a Cybertruck, you will get $1000 off your purchase price. At this moment, Tesla is offering a year of free Supercharging with the purchase of a new Model 3 Premium or Performance.

Arthur Frederick (Fritz) Hasler, PhD, former leader of NASA Goddard Space Flight Center Scientific Visualization & Analysis Laboratory (creator of this iconic image), and avid CleanTechnica reader. Also: Research Meteorologist (Emeritus) at NASA GSFC, Adjunct Professor at Viterbo University On-Line Studies, PSIA L2 Certified Alpine Ski Instructor at Brighton Utah Ski School.

Nile Rodgers Defends AI Use in Music Video: ‘We Created Everything … The AI Did What We Told It to Do’

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Nile Rodgers recently shared a snippet of his upcoming video for a new version of “The Christmas Song,” his 2020 charity collaboration with Rebecca Ferguson, proudly revealing it used AI, which fans took issue with. On Thursday, he defended his use of the technology.

Earlier this week, he shared an “AI capture” of the project. “#working on a #great #art project that’s next #level. This is the start of the process. This AI capture was very thorough and I’m so excited about the finished project,” he wrote. “When I saw the Eagles at The Sphere in Vegas I envisioned stories with incredible environments & music. It’s future time!”

Shortly afterward, however, people began commenting on their disappointment with him using AI. One person wrote in the IG comments, “There’s better way to preserve your legacy.” Rodgers responded, “Wait to you see it before you make judgement. You’re imagining the worst instead of imagining the best.” The person responded back: “I don’t have to imagine my friend, look what AI has done to art in general yet alone music. Promoting it in such a big way is not the way to go.”

On Wednesday, Rodgers shared photo stills from the project, and the backlash continued. “We need happiness and healing in our world,” he captioned the carousel. “When I had my 1st bought of #cancer it was a child who coached me and gave me #courage to fight. I know a lot of children who are bedridden and ill. Their main form of entertainment is the iPad or other such devices. I produced ‘The Christmas Song’ to give them a little #joy during the holiday season.”He added: “We gave the #video a fun twist on the story line…and in the process expanded a new creative family for me. Over the next few weeks while we finish this video I’ll show bits and pieces while we rush to get it ready by November 20. Peace, love, and good health for the holidays.” Trending Stories Appeals Court Says Lawsuit Over ‘The Pitt’ Can Proceed, Cites ‘Numerous’ Similarities to ‘ER’ Nicki Minaj Is Going Down With Trump’s Ship After Nearly Every Candidate She Backed Lost As Nicki Minaj Rallied for Trump's GOP, Her Lawyers Told Court She Still Owes Them $295,000 Adam Lambert on His Future With Queen, New Music, and Why the Show Must Go On

Several commenters on his post from Wednesday took him to task for using generative AI as well. Rodgers defended its use, telling one commenter: “We created everything. Mood boards, story boards and did it the same way we’d do a film. The AI did what we told it to do and we painstakingly created every second you’ll see on the screen… If this were a traditional film it would’ve cost about $100,000.”

Rodgers told another commenter that “it’s somewhat hypocritical to say you’re anti AI and you’re using it all the time because it makes your life convenient,” he wrote. “I used to work with Microsoft and Apple. I’m very aware of the things they do when they have new tech that consumers like yourself adopt its usage. Love to you. I respect all opinions.”Rodgers has previously shown support for AI. In 2024, he told Euronews, “So, I think using AI-generated vocals to copy another person’s sound is horrible, but using AI to artistically express yourself and make better music, I think, is fantastic. The way I want to look at AI is from the positive side. I want to look at it as just another color on your palette,” he said, likening it to other tools. He cited how when drum machines and synthesizers first came out, people were saying it “wasn’t real music,” and how auto-tune helped shape the pop landscape. “I think that creativity comes from the artist. Now, if you have a tool to help you become more creative, there’s nothing wrong with that.” Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

PSG: the reason for the absence of Mika Godts in training

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Scorer for the first time in his new colors on Monday against OL (2-0), Mika Godts was absent from PSG training this Thursday morning. No injury for the Belgian winger, but rather very good news since the former Ajax Amsterdam player is preparing to become a father.

Marquinhos, Ousmane Dembélé and Nuno Mendes, injured in the thigh, in addition to Khvicha Kvaratskhelia, victim of a ruptured cruciate ligament, were the other absentees of the day. They continue their care. Quentin Ndjantou, David Boly and Alessandro Longoni were present. PSG faces Le Havre tomorrow evening, on the 9th day of Ligue 1.

Dropbox acquires Tigris Data for $350M, releases new AI productivity features

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Dropbox Inc. is acquiring cloud storage startup Tigris Data Inc. for $350 million.

The company announced the deal today in conjunction with a major update to its product portfolio. It introduced DBX Agent Cloud, a service that artificial intelligence agents can use to store their data. Dropbox is also adding new AI productivity features to its flagship file sharing platform.

Sunnyvale, California-based Tigris Data offers an object storage service that runs across multiple public clouds. The company says that it enables applications to read and write several times faster than competing offerings. Furthermore, the service includes several features designed to optimize AI workloads.

AI agents often run in sandboxes that include, among other resources, the data they need to carry out their work. Loading data into a sandbox can be a relatively time-consuming process, which slows agent performance. Tigris Data offers a feature called bucket forms that speeds up the workflow. Furthermore, it prevents agents from modifying information that is used by other workloads.

Tigris Data’s service monitors the locations from which agents access records. When it detects a new location, it moves a copy of the records to a nearby server in order to reduce latency. The service caches the most frequently used files on high-speed storage infrastructure to further reduce access times.

Dropbox said Tigris Data will support its push into the AI infrastructure market. The company debuted its first entry into that product category, DBX Agent Cloud, in conjunction with the acquisition announcement. Like Tigris Data’s service, it’s an S3-compatible object storage offering optimized for AI agents.

The storage stalwart plans to add a file system capability further down the line. Object storage is best suited for unstructured datasets, while file systems are used to store records that are organized into folders. Dropbox plans to release the technology alongside “content processing capabilities.” The company says that those features will make it easier for AI models to process information kept in DBX Agent Cloud.

The offering is rolling out alongside a major overhaul of Dropbox’s flagship file sharing platform. An enhanced search bar will enable users to look for images by entering a description of the file they seek.

Furthermore, Dropbox can now respond to questions about a file by generating a natural language summary. A worker could, for example, request an overview of the changes that colleagues have made to an important folder over the past week.

Dropbox can not only edit questions about files but also edit them. The company says that users can have the built-in AI models change a file’s format, move it to a different folder or combine it with other records into a PDF document. Several other actions are supported as well. Users can mix and match actions into multistep workflows.

The new AI productivity features are initially available to customers in the U.S., the U.K. and Australia with Standard and Advanced subscriptions. Dropbox plans to expand access next year.

Amy Winehouse’s ‘Back to Black’ Reissue Sparks Top 10 Chart Return

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The late singer's sophomore set makes a top 10 splash on four charts.

Amy Winehouse’s Grammy-winning Back to Black album impacts the top 10 of four Billboard charts, following its 20th-anniversary reissue. The album re-enters the top 10 of Indie Store Album Sales (No. 3), Vinyl Albums (No. 4), Top Album Sales (No. 7) and Top R&B Albums (No. 7) (all charts dated Nov. 7). The project also jumps back onto the overall all-genre Billboard 200 at No. 42.

Back to Black peaked at No. 2 on the Billboard 200 in 2008, following its big night at that year’s Grammy Awards, where Winehouse won record of the year, song of the year, best female pop vocal performance (for the album’s “Rehab”), best new artist and best pop vocal album (for Back to Black).

For its 20th anniversary, the album was reissued in a variety of options on Oct. 23, including an expanded version with previously unreleased b-sides, demos and a live concert.

Back to Black marked the second and final studio album from Winehouse before her death in 2011, and spun off the Billboard Hot 100-charting singles “You Know I’m No Good” and the No. 9 hit “Rehab.”

In the tracking week ending Oct. 29, Back to Black earned 16,000 equivalent album units (up 185%) in the United States, according to Luminate. Of that figure, 11,000 was in pure album sales (up 885%), with the remainder driven by streaming activity and track sales. (All versions of the album, old and new, are combined for tracking and charting purposes.)

Back to Black is among seven debuts or reentries in the top 10 of the latest Top Album Sales chart. The highest debut of the week belongs to Anderson .Paak and Cordae’s collaborative project Heavy Is the Crown (No. 2), while My Chemical Romance’s The Black Parade jumps 29-1 following its own 20th anniversary reissue.

"You Don't Want To Interrupt The Player's Experience" - Here's Why Zelda's Switch 2 Remake Cut Some Mario References

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Nintendo is known in recent times for dropping Star Fox (and even Pikmin) into the Super Mario universe, but with the Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake on the Switch 2, it decided to take a little more care.

In the castle courtyard, where Link meets with Zelda, you might have noticed how the character portraits for Mario and friends are not in the Switch 2 version of the game. If you are wondering about the thought process behind this, Zelda series producer Eiji Aonuma has explained how the team didn't want to break the immersion.

Here's what he had to say during the same chat with Game Informer:

Eiji Aonuma: "Certainly, at the time, when we put those [portraits] there, I don't think anybody thought it was too big a deal. Maybe there was a more carefree or comfortable approach to that sort of thing. But looking at it now, like if a player is coming through that area, and given the overall way that we're, you know, creating this world and giving the player that form of expression, if they look up and they think, "Why is Yoshi there?” We don't want that moment to interrupt their experience of the world in that way. So we're feeling a little bit differently about it this time."

If you recall, the 3DS game also had Skyward Sword art hidden in certain locations, and this is also gone from the Switch 2 remake. According to Aonuma, the same reasoning applies here - the team didn't want to "interrupt the player's experience by seeing something that's unrelated to what they're currently doing in the game".

Although these easter eggs are gone, certain references to the Mario series remain intact as they are part of the character designs:

"You will remember that there is one design element on Talon with Bowser on it, and that's part of the original design of the character. So we didn't change that. That stays as is."

There’s no reason they need to treat an all-time classic as a museum piece, because the original game will always be a masterpiece. Better to iterate upon it with fresh ideas and convenient tweaks than to declare any changes off-limits because of Nostalgia™.

The only major thing I would have done differently is to preserve the more poetic and succinct quality of the original English translation. In general immersion is not something I have ever remotely cared about and I'd a lot rather just keep the random goofy references, but it's also not exactly a dealbreaker.

WhatsApp’s AI-powered photo expansion tool starts rolling out on iOS

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Select WhatsApp users on TestFlight and in the main app are now seeing the option to expand an image in the app’s photo editor. Here are the details.

iPhone users with access to Apple Intelligence have a native AI-powered photo expansion tool called Extend, which lets users pinch or drag to expand an image beyond its original borders.

This feature, which is also present on AI chatbots such as ChatGPT and image-editing platforms like Photoshop, is now also making its way into WhatsApp.

According to WABetaInfo, the platform is rolling out a Meta AI-powered image expansion tool, though it is currently limited to select users, primarily those in WhatsApp’s beta program via TestFlight.

When users select a photo in their chats, WhatsApp opens the drawing editor. Here, they can choose to apply some AI effect to improve it. Among these, users can find a new option to expand their photos. Once users select this option, Meta AI takes a few seconds to analyze the photo and generate the expanded version. Once the process is complete, WhatsApp shows the expanded photo in the drawing editor, and users can choose to send it. Note that this feature is also compatible with status updates.

The site notes that if the user isn’t happy with the result, they can tap an undo button to revert to the original and then try using the tool again to generate a different result.

As is usually the case with beta features on WhatsApp, there is no telling when it might become widely available.

That said, given that it has also rolled out to users in the Android beta and some users on the public iOS version already have access, a broader rollout might not be too far off.

To check out WABetaInfo’s full report, follow this link.
